Creation, like\\n28: // config update, is done in two phases: the first phase consumes the\\n29: // QueuingConfig and the second consumes the DispatchingConfig.  They\\n30: // are separated so that errors from the first phase can be found\\n31: // before committing to a concurrency allotment for the second.\\n32: type QueueSetFactory interface {\\n33: \\t// BeginConstruction does the first phase of creating a QueueSet.\\n34: \\t// The RatioedGaugePair observes number of requests,\\n35: \\t// execution covering just the regular phase.\\n36: \\t// The denominator for the waiting phase is\\n37: \\t// max(1, QueuingConfig.QueueLengthLimit) X max(1, QueuingConfig.DesiredNumQueues).\\n38: \\t// The RatioedGauge observes number of seats occupied through all phases of execution.\\n39: \\t// The denominator for all the ratioed concurrency gauges is supplied later in the DispatchingConfig.\\n40: \\t// The Gauge observes the seat demand (executing + queued seats).\\n41: \\tBeginConstruction(QueuingConfig, metrics.RatioedGaugePair, metrics.RatioedGauge, metrics.Gauge) (QueueSetCompleter, error)\\n42: }\\n43: \\n44: // QueueSetCompleter finishes the two-step process of creating or\\n45: // reconfiguring a QueueSet\\n46: type QueueSetCompleter interface {\\n47: \\t// Complete returns a QueueSet configured by the given\\n48: \\t// dispatching configuration.\\n49: \\tComplete(DispatchingConfig) QueueSet\\n50: }\\n51: \\n52: // QueueSet is the abstraction for the queuing and dispatching\\n53: // functionality of one non-exempt priority level. Some day we may have connections between priority levels, but\\n58: // today is not that day.\\n59: type QueueSet interface {\\n60: \\t// BeginConfigChange starts the two-step process of updating the\\n61: \\t// configuration.  No change is made until Complete is called.  If\\n62: \\t// `C := X.BeginConstruction(q)` then `C.Complete(d)` returns the\\n63: \\t// same value `X`.  If the QueuingConfig's DesiredNumQueues field\\n64: \\t// is zero then the other queuing-specific config parameters are\\n65: \\t// not changed, so that the queues continue draining as before.\\n66: \\t// In any case, reconfiguration does not discard any queue unless\\n67: \\t// and until it is undesired and empty.\\n68: \\tBeginConfigChange(QueuingConfig) (QueueSetCompleter, error)\\n69: \\n70: \\t// IsIdle returns a bool indicating whether the QueueSet was idle\\n71: \\t// at the moment of the return.  Idle means the QueueSet has zero\\n72: \\t// requests queued and zero executing.  This bit can change only\\n73: \\t// (1) during a call to StartRequest and (2) during a call to\\n74: \\t// Request::Finish.  In the latter case idleness can only change\\n75: \\t// from false to true.\\n76: \\tIsIdle() bool\\n77: \\n78: \\t// StartRequest begins the process of handling a request.  If the\\n79: \\t// request gets queued and the number of queues is greater than 1\\n80: \\t// then StartRequest uses the given hashValue as the source of\\n81: \\t// entropy as it shuffle-shards the request into a queue.  The\\n82: \\t// descr1 and descr2 values play no role in the logic but appear\\n83: \\t// in log messages.  This method always returns quickly (without\\n84: \\t// waiting for the request to be dequeued).  If this method\\n85: \\t// returns a nil Request value then caller should reject the\\n86: \\t// request and the returned bool indicates whether the QueueSet\\n87: \\t// was idle at the moment of the return.  Otherwise idle==false\\n88: \\t// and the client must call the Finish method of the Request\\n89: \\t// exactly once.\\n90: \\tStartRequest(ctx context.Context, width *request.WorkEstimate, hashValue uint64, flowDistinguisher, fsName string, descr1, descr2 interface{}, queueNoteFn QueueNoteFn) (req Request, idle bool)\\n91: \\n92: \\t// Dump saves and returns the instant internal state of the queue-set.\\n93: \\t// Note that dumping process will stop the queue-set from proceeding\\n94: \\t// any requests.\\n95: \\t// For debugging only.\\n96: \\tDump(includeRequestDetails bool) debug.QueueSetDump\\n97: }\\n98: \\n99: // QueueNoteFn is called when a request enters and leaves a queue\\n100: type QueueNoteFn func(inQueue bool)\\n101: \\n102: // Request represents the remainder of the handling of one request\\n103: type Request interface {\\n104: \\t// Finish determines whether to execute or reject the request and\\n105: \\t// invokes `execute` if the decision is to execute the request.\\n106: \\t// The returned `idle bool` value indicates whether the QueueSet\\n107: \\t// was idle when the value was calculated, but might no longer be\\n108: \\t// accurate by the time the client examines that value.\\n109: \\tFinish(execute func()) (idle bool)\\n110: }\\n111: \\n112: // QueuingConfig defines the configuration of the queuing aspect of a QueueSet.\\n113: type QueuingConfig struct {\\n114: \\t// Name is used to identify a queue set, allowing for descriptive information about its intended use\\n115: \\tName string\\n116: \\n117: \\t// DesiredNumQueues is the number of queues that the API says\\n118: \\t// should exist now.  This may be non-positive, in which case\\n119: \\t// QueueLengthLimit, and HandSize are ignored.\\n120: \\t// A value of zero means to respect the ConcurrencyLimit of the DispatchingConfig.\\n121: \\t// A negative value means to always dispatch immediately upon arrival\\n122: \\t// (i.e., the requests are \\\"exempt\\\" from limitation).\\n123: \\tDesiredNumQueues int\\n124: \\n125: \\t// QueueLengthLimit is the maximum number of requests that may be waiting in a given queue at a time\\n126: \\tQueueLengthLimit int\\n127: \\n128: \\t// HandSize is a parameter of shuffle sharding.  Upon arrival of a request, a queue is chosen by randomly\\n129: \\t// dealing a \\\"hand\\\" of this many queues and then picking one of minimum length.\\n130: \\tHandSize int\\n131: }\\n132: \\n133: They\n30: // are separated so that errors from the first phase can be found\n31: // before committing to a concurrency allotment for the second.\n32: type QueueSetFactory interface {\n33: \t// BeginConstruction does the first phase of creating a QueueSet.\n34: \t// The RatioedGaugePair observes number of requests,\n35: \t// execution covering just the regular phase.\n36: \t// The denominator for the waiting phase is\n37: \t// max(1, QueuingConfig.QueueLengthLimit) X max(1, QueuingConfig.DesiredNumQueues).\n38: \t// The RatioedGauge observes number of seats occupied through all phases of execution.\n39: \t// The denominator for all the ratioed concurrency gauges is supplied later in the DispatchingConfig.\n40: \t// The Gauge observes the seat demand (executing + queued seats).\n41: \tBeginConstruction(QueuingConfig, metrics.RatioedGaugePair, metrics.RatioedGauge, metrics.Gauge) (QueueSetCompleter, error)\n42: }\n43: \n44: // QueueSetCompleter finishes the two-step process of creating or\n45: // reconfiguring a QueueSet\n46: type QueueSetCompleter interface {\n47: \t// Complete returns a QueueSet configured by the given\n48: \t// dispatching configuration.\n49: \tComplete(DispatchingConfig) QueueSet\n50: }\n51: \n52: // QueueSet is the abstraction for the queuing and dispatching\n53: // functionality of one non-exempt priority level.  It covers the\n54: // functionality described in the \"Assignment to a Queue\", \"Queuing\",\n55: // and \"Dispatching\" sections of\n56: // https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/blob/master/keps/sig-api-machinery/1040-priority-and-fairness/README.md\n57: // .  Some day we may have connections between priority levels, but\n58: // today is not that day.\n59: type QueueSet interface {\n60: \t// BeginConfigChange starts the two-step process of updating the\n61: \t// configuration.  No change is made until Complete is called.  If\n62: \t// `C := X.BeginConstruction(q)` then `C.Complete(d)` returns the\n63: \t// same value `X`.  If the QueuingConfig's DesiredNumQueues field\n64: \t// is zero then the other queuing-specific config parameters are\n65: \t// not changed, so that the queues continue draining as before.\n66: \t// In any case, reconfiguration does not discard any queue unless\n67: \t// and until it is undesired and empty.\n68: \tBeginConfigChange(QueuingConfig) (QueueSetCompleter, error)\n69: \n70: \t// IsIdle returns a bool indicating whether the QueueSet was idle\n71: \t// at the moment of the return.  Idle means the QueueSet has zero\n72: \t// requests queued and zero executing.  This bit can change only\n73: \t// (1) during a call to StartRequest and (2) during a call to\n74: \t// Request::Finish.  In the latter case idleness can only change\n75: \t// from false to true.\n76: \tIsIdle() bool\n77: \n78: \t// StartRequest begins the process of handling a request.  If the\n79: \t// request gets queued and the number of queues is greater than 1\n80: \t// then StartRequest uses the given hashValue as the source of\n81: \t// entropy as it shuffle-shards the request into a queue.  The\n82: \t// descr1 and descr2 values play no role in the logic but appear\n83: \t// in log messages.  This method always returns quickly (without\n84: \t// waiting for the request to be dequeued).  If this method\n85: \t// returns a nil Request value then caller should reject the\n86: \t// request and the returned bool indicates whether the QueueSet\n87: \t// was idle at the moment of the return.  Otherwise idle==false\n88: \t// and the client must call the Finish method of the Request\n89: \t// exactly once.\n90: \tStartRequest(ctx context.Context, width *request.WorkEstimate, hashValue uint64, flowDistinguisher, fsName string, descr1, descr2 interface{}, queueNoteFn QueueNoteFn) (req Request, idle bool)\n91: \n92: \t// Dump saves and returns the instant internal state of the queue-set.\n93: \t// Note that dumping process will stop the queue-set from proceeding\n94: \t// any requests.\n95: \t// For debugging only.\n96: \tDump(includeRequestDetails bool) debug.QueueSetDump\n97: }\n98: \n99: // QueueNoteFn is called when a request enters and leaves a queue\n100: type QueueNoteFn func(inQueue bool)\n101: \n102: // Request represents the remainder of the handling of one request\n103: type Request interface {\n104: \t// Finish determines whether to execute or reject the request and\n105: \t// invokes `execute` if the decision is to execute the request.\n106: \t// The returned `idle bool` value indicates whether the QueueSet\n107: \t// was idle when the value was calculated, but might no longer be\n108: \t// accurate by the time the client examines that value.\n109: \tFinish(execute func()) (idle bool)\n110: }\n111: \n112: // QueuingConfig defines the configuration of the queuing aspect of a QueueSet.\n113: type QueuingConfig struct {\n114: \t// Name is used to identify a queue set, allowing for descriptive information about its intended use\n115: \tName string\n116: \n117: \t// DesiredNumQueues is the number of queues that the API says\n118: \t// should exist now.  This may be non-positive, in which case\n119: \t// QueueLengthLimit, and HandSize are ignored.\n120: \t// A value of zero means to respect the ConcurrencyLimit of the DispatchingConfig.\n121: \t// A negative value means to always dispatch immediately upon arrival\n122: \t// (i.e., the requests are \"exempt\" from limitation).\n123: \tDesiredNumQueues int\n124: \n125: \t// QueueLengthLimit is the maximum number of requests that may be waiting in a given queue at a time\n126: \tQueueLengthLimit int\n127: \n128: \t// HandSize is a parameter of shuffle sharding.  Upon arrival of a request, a queue is chosen by randomly\n129: \t// dealing a \"hand\" of this many queues and then picking one of minimum length.\n130: \tHandSize int\n131: }\n132: \n133: // DispatchingConfig defines the configuration of the dispatching aspect of a QueueSet.\n134: type DispatchingConfig struct {\n135: \t// ConcurrencyLimit is the maximum number of requests of this QueueSet that may be executing at a time\n136: \tConcurrencyLimit int\n137: \n138: \t// ConcurrencyDenominator is used in relative metrics of concurrency.\n139: \t// It equals ConcurrencyLimit except when that is zero.\n140: \tConcurrencyDenominator int\n141: }\n142: ","url":"https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/github.com/sg-benchmarks/kubernetes--stripped@6d5678ae074d17945f174da2b064603131370a5f/-/blob/staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/interface.go"}}}

For a configController, the\n94: // suffix \"ReadLocked\" stipulates a read lock while just \"Locked\"\n95: // stipulates a full lock. The\n340: \t\t\t// concern is that we might, in some future release, want\n341: \t\t\t// different behavior than is implemented now. One of the\n342: \t\t\t// hardest questions is how does an operator roll out the\n343: \t\t\t// new release in a cluster with multiple kube-apiservers\n344: \t\t\t// --- in a way that works no matter what servers crash\n345: \t\t\t// and restart when. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_0178UM476CinthsQ27iRJSYt","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01NC6uLWW11a86simSFf1UQJ","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# Kubernetes APF QueueSet Deep-Dive: Algorithmic Analysis & Architecture\n\n## Executive Summary\n\nThe Kubernetes API Priority and Fairness (APF) subsystem implements **Fair Queuing for Server Requests** (FFQ), a sophisticated flow-control mechanism that isolates request behavior across priority levels and flow schemas. The **QueueSet** is the core component responsible for queuing and dispatching individual requests within a priority level, using virtual-time-based fair queuing to ensure that high-variance request patterns don't starve lower-priority flows.\n\nThis document provides an algorithmic analysis of QueueSet behavior, end-to-end control flow, and the fairness tradeoffs embedded in the design.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Subsystem Purpose, Boundaries, and Dependencies\n\n### Purpose\n\nThe APF QueueSet addresses a critical problem in multi-tenant or shared API servers: **head-of-line blocking** and **uncontrolled bursting** at the per-priority-level scale. Without QueueSet:\n- A single misbehaving user (via one FlowSchema) could create an unbounded queue of requests that starves all other flows at that priority level.\n- Virtual time doesn't progress fairly when requests have variable processing duration.\n- No mechanism exists to enforce **seat-based concurrency limits** (where a request can occupy multiple execution slots based on estimated work).\n\nThe QueueSet solves this by:\n1. **Shuffle-sharding** incoming requests into sub-queues to reduce collision probability.\n2. **Dispatching** using virtual time to ensure fair progress (weighted by request work estimates).\n3. **Enforcing concurrency limits** across multiple \"seats\" (execution parallelism units).\n\n### Boundaries\n\n**Upstream Dependencies:**\n- **APF Filter** (`apf_filter.go`): Entry point that calls `Handle()` on the APF Interface.\n- **APF Controller** (`apf_controller.go`): Manages PriorityLevelConfiguration and FlowSchema objects; creates and reconfigures QueueSets.\n- **Request classification**: FlowSchema and DistinguisherMethod determine which QueueSet a request enters.\n\n**Downstream Responsibilities:**\n- Each **non-exempt PriorityLevelConfiguration** gets one QueueSet instance.\n- **Exempt** requests (negative DesiredNumQueues) bypass all queuing and proceed directly.\n- QueueSet returns a `Request` handle; the filter calls `Request.Finish(execute)` to dispatch or reject.\n\n**Internal Scope:**\n- QueueSet is **not responsible for** flow-schema matching, rate-limiting above the priority level, or inter-priority-level borrowing (controller handles that).\n- QueueSet is **responsible for** all queuing, shuffle-sharding, virtual-time bookkeeping, and per-queue dispatching within a single priority level.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Key Components and Responsibility Split\n\n### 2.1 QueueSet Interface (`interface.go:59-97`)\n\nThe QueueSet contract defines:\n\n```\ntype QueueSet interface {\n    BeginConfigChange(QueuingConfig) (QueueSetCompleter, error)\n    IsIdle() bool\n    StartRequest(ctx, width, hashValue, flowDistinguisher, fsName, descr1, descr2, queueNoteFn) (Request, bool)\n    Dump(includeRequestDetails bool) debug.QueueSetDump\n}\n```\n\n- **`StartRequest`** is the hot path: called on every incoming request, returns a `Request` handle or `nil` if rejected.\n- **`Finish`** on the returned Request decides execution vs. rejection.\n- **Two-phase config** (`BeginConfigChange` + `Complete`) allows validation before applying changes.\n\n### 2.2 QueueSet Implementation (`queueset/queueset.go`)\n\n#### Struct Fields (Read-Only Before Lock)\n\n```go\ntype queueSet struct {\n    clock                    eventclock.Interface\n    estimatedServiceDuration time.Duration  // 3ms default; used to compute final work\n    reqsGaugePair            metrics.RatioedGaugePair\n    execSeatsGauge           metrics.RatioedGauge\n    seatDemandIntegrator     metrics.Gauge\n    promiseFactory           promiseFactory\n    lock                     sync.Mutex\n    // ... mutable fields follow\n}\n```\n\n#### Struct Fields (Guarded by Lock)\n\n```go\ntype queueSet struct {\n    // ... read-only fields ...\n    lock                     sync.Mutex\n\n    qCfg                     fq.QueuingConfig      // num queues, queue length limit, hand size\n    dCfg                     fq.DispatchingConfig  // concurrency limit\n    dealer                   *shufflesharding.Dealer\n    queues                   []*queue              // array of sub-queues\n\n    currentR                 fqrequest.SeatSeconds // virtual time (progress meter)\n    lastRealTime             time.Time\n    robinIndex               int                   // round-robin dispatch position\n\n    totRequestsWaiting       int\n    totRequestsExecuting     int\n    requestsExecutingSet     sets.Set[*request]    // used when queues is empty\n    totSeatsInUse            int\n    totSeatsWaiting          int\n\n    enqueues                 int  // counter for shuffle-sharding offset\n    totRequestsDispatched    int\n    totRequestsRejected      int\n    totRequestsTimedout      int\n    totRequestsCancelled     int\n}\n```\n\n**Key insight**: The queueSet tracks both **request count** (for backward compatibility metrics) and **seat count** (for work-aware concurrency control).\n\n#### Queue Sub-Structure (`types.go:93-110`)\n\nEach queue is a FIFO with virtual-time bookkeeping:\n\n```go\ntype queue struct {\n    requestsWaiting fifo                    // FIFO list of waiting requests\n    nextDispatchR   fqrequest.SeatSeconds   // virtual time for next dispatch\n    requestsExecuting sets.Set[*request]    // executing requests in this queue\n    index           int                     // position in queueSet.queues\n    seatsInUse      int                     // total seats occupied by executing requests\n}\n```\n\n### 2.3 Request Wrapper (`types.go:31-83`)\n\n```go\ntype request struct {\n    ctx context.Context\n    qs  *queueSet\n\n    flowDistinguisher string\n    fsName            string\n    queue             *queue\n\n    workEstimate      completedWorkEstimate // initial, final, total seats\n    decision          promise.WriteOnce     // executes or rejects\n    arrivalTime       time.Time\n    arrivalR          fqrequest.SeatSeconds // virtual time at arrival\n\n    startTime         time.Time\n    waitStarted       bool\n    removeFromQueueLocked removeFromFIFOFunc\n\n    descr1, descr2 interface{}\n    queueNoteFn    fq.QueueNoteFn\n}\n```\n\nThe `decision` field is a **WriteOnce promise**: set exactly once (by dispatcher), read by the request handler.\n\n### 2.4 Shuffle-Sharding Dealer (`queueset.go:583-612`)\n\nShuffle-sharding picks the **least-loaded queue** among a random hand:\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) shuffleShardLocked(hashValue uint64, descr1, descr2 interface{}) int {\n    hand := qs.dealer.DealIntoHand(hashValue, backHand[:])\n    handSize := len(hand)\n    offset := qs.enqueues % handSize  // offset for additional diversity\n\n    minQueueSeatSeconds := MaxSeatSeconds\n    for i := 0; i < handSize; i++ {\n        queueIdx := hand[(offset+i)%handSize]\n        queue := qs.queues[queueIdx]\n        queueSum := queue.requestsWaiting.QueueSum()\n\n        if queueSum.TotalWorkSum < minQueueSeatSeconds {\n            minQueueSeatSeconds = queueSum.TotalWorkSum\n            bestQueueIdx = queueIdx\n        }\n    }\n    return bestQueueIdx\n}\n```\n\n**Insight**: Choosing the **least-loaded queue** (by total work, not request count) ensures balanced work distribution. The hash-based hand-dealing reduces collisions for flows with similar hash values.\n\n### 2.5 Virtual Time Mechanism (`queueset.go:461-479, 489-522`)\n\nVirtual time is the **cornerstone of fair queuing**.\n\n#### Time Sync (called at lock acquisition)\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) syncTimeLocked(ctx context.Context) {\n    realNow := qs.clock.Now()\n    timeSinceLast := realNow.Sub(qs.lastRealTime)\n    qs.lastRealTime = realNow\n\n    prevR := qs.currentR\n    incrR := SeatsTimesDuration(qs.getVirtualTimeRatioLocked(), timeSinceLast)\n    qs.currentR = prevR + incrR\n\n    if qs.currentR >= highR {  // overflow prevention\n        qs.advanceEpoch(ctx, realNow, incrR)\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Virtual time increments at a rate proportional to:**\n- **Number of active queues** (those with waiting or executing requests)\n- **Concurrency limit** vs. **seat demand**\n\nThe ratio: `min(totalSeatsRequested, ConcurrencyLimit) / activeQueues`\n\nThis ensures that if all queues demand work equally, virtual time advances at the same rate for each.\n\n#### Epoch Management (Overflow Handling)\n\nWhen `currentR` exceeds `highR = rDecrement + rDecrement/2`:\n- Subtract `rDecrement` from `currentR` and all queue/request arrival times.\n- This prevents 64-bit overflow while maintaining relative ordering.\n\n---\n\n## 3. End-to-End Control and Request Flow\n\n### 3.1 Request Arrival: `StartRequest` (3 Steps)\n\n#### Step 0: Zero Queues (Direct Dispatch)\n\nIf `DesiredNumQueues < 1` (exempt or zero queues):\n- Check if request seats can fit: `totSeatsInUse + width > ConcurrencyLimit` → **reject**.\n- Otherwise, call `dispatchSansQueueLocked()` and return the request immediately.\n- No queuing overhead.\n\n#### Step 1: Shuffle-Shard and Enqueue\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) StartRequest(ctx context.Context, workEstimate *WorkEstimate, hashValue uint64, ...) (Request, bool) {\n    qs.lockAndSyncTime(ctx)  // update virtual time\n    defer qs.lock.Unlock()\n\n    queueIdx := qs.shuffleShardLocked(hashValue, descr1, descr2)\n    req := qs.shuffleShardAndRejectOrEnqueueLocked(...)\n    if req == nil {\n        return nil, qs.isIdleLocked()  // rejected: queue full AND at concurrency limit\n    }\n\n    // Step 2: Dispatch as much as possible\n    qs.dispatchAsMuchAsPossibleLocked()\n    return req, false\n}\n```\n\n**Enqueue Logic** (`rejectOrEnqueueToBoundLocked`):\n- Reject if **both**: `totSeatsInUse >= ConcurrencyLimit` **AND** `queueLength >= QueueLengthLimit`.\n- Otherwise, enqueue and initialize `queue.nextDispatchR = currentR` if queue was empty.\n\n**Metrics Updated:**\n- `totRequestsWaiting++`\n- `totSeatsWaiting += req.MaxSeats()`\n- `reqsGaugePair.RequestsWaiting.Add(1)`\n- `seatDemandIntegrator.Set(totSeatsInUse + totSeatsWaiting)`\n\n#### Step 2: Dispatch Loop\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) dispatchAsMuchAsPossibleLocked() {\n    for qs.totRequestsWaiting != 0 &&\n        qs.totSeatsInUse < qs.dCfg.ConcurrencyLimit &&\n        qs.dispatchLocked() {\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Loop condition**: While there are waiting requests, concurrency available, and we can find a dispatchable request.\n\n### 3.2 Dispatch: Fair Queuing Selection\n\n#### Queue Selection (`findDispatchQueueToBoundLocked`)\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) findDispatchQueueToBoundLocked() (*queue, *request) {\n    minVirtualFinish := MaxSeatSeconds\n\n    for range qs.queues {\n        qs.robinIndex = (qs.robinIndex + 1) % len(qs.queues)\n        queue := qs.queues[qs.robinIndex]\n\n        oldestWaiting, _ := queue.requestsWaiting.Peek()\n        if oldestWaiting != nil {\n            // virtual finish time = queue's start point + request's work\n            currentVirtualFinish := queue.nextDispatchR + oldestWaiting.totalWork()\n\n            if currentVirtualFinish < minVirtualFinish {\n                minVirtualFinish = currentVirtualFinish\n                minQueue = queue\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    return minQueue, minQueue.requestsWaiting.Peek()[0]\n}\n```\n\n**Key property**: Pick the queue whose **oldest request** has the **minimum virtual finish time**.\n\nThis ensures:\n- Requests finish in order of virtual time (FIFO within each queue).\n- Queues with less cumulative work get to execute sooner.\n- High-variance requests don't starve others.\n\n#### Request Dispatch (`dispatchLocked`)\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) dispatchLocked() bool {\n    queue, request := qs.findDispatchQueueToBoundLocked()\n    if queue == nil {\n        return false\n    }\n\n    qs.totRequestsWaiting--\n    qs.totSeatsWaiting -= request.MaxSeats()\n    request.NoteQueued(false)  // notify filter\n\n    // Set the decision to EXECUTE\n    if !request.decision.Set(decisionExecute) {\n        return true  // context was cancelled; request already abandoned\n    }\n\n    request.startTime = qs.clock.Now()\n\n    // Move from waiting to executing\n    qs.totRequestsExecuting++\n    qs.totSeatsInUse += request.MaxSeats()\n    queue.requestsExecuting = queue.requestsExecuting.Insert(request)\n    queue.seatsInUse += request.MaxSeats()\n\n    // Advance virtual finish time\n    queue.nextDispatchR += request.totalWork()\n\n    return true\n}\n```\n\n**Virtual time advance**: `queue.nextDispatchR += request.totalWork()`\n\nThis is critical: the next request in this queue won't dispatch until its virtual finish time equals or exceeds the current queue's `nextDispatchR`.\n\n### 3.3 Request Execution and Completion: `Request.Finish`\n\n#### Waiting Phase\n\n```go\nfunc (req *request) wait() (bool, bool) {\n    decisionAny := req.decision.Get()  // blocks until dispatcher sets it\n    qs := req.qs\n    qs.lockAndSyncTime(req.ctx)\n    defer qs.lock.Unlock()\n\n    switch decisionAny {\n    case decisionExecute:\n        return true, false\n    case decisionCancel:\n        // request context timed out or was cancelled while waiting\n        if req.removeFromQueueLocked() != nil {\n            qs.totRequestsWaiting--\n            qs.totSeatsWaiting -= req.MaxSeats()\n            qs.totRequestsCancelled++\n        }\n        return false, qs.isIdleLocked()\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Context Cancellation Handling**: If the client cancels while waiting, the request is removed from the queue before it starts executing. The `decision.Set()` call in `dispatchLocked` may fail if the context is already done.\n\n#### Finish and Re-Dispatch\n\n```go\nfunc (req *request) Finish(execFn func()) bool {\n    exec, idle := req.wait()\n    if !exec {\n        return idle  // was rejected or cancelled\n    }\n\n    func() {\n        defer func() {\n            idle = req.qs.finishRequestAndDispatchAsMuchAsPossible(req)\n        }()\n        execFn()  // execute the user's request\n    }()\n\n    return idle\n}\n\nfunc (qs *queueSet) finishRequestAndDispatchAsMuchAsPossible(req *request) bool {\n    qs.lockAndSyncTime(req.ctx)\n    defer qs.lock.Unlock()\n\n    // Remove from executing\n    queue := req.queue\n    if queue != nil {\n        queue.requestsExecuting = queue.requestsExecuting.Delete(req)\n        queue.seatsInUse -= req.MaxSeats()\n    } else {\n        qs.requestsExecutingSet = qs.requestsExecutingSet.Delete(req)\n    }\n    qs.totRequestsExecuting--\n    qs.totSeatsInUse -= req.MaxSeats()\n\n    // Update metrics\n    metrics.AddSeatConcurrencyInUse(qs.qCfg.Name, req.fsName, -req.MaxSeats())\n\n    // Try to dispatch more\n    qs.dispatchAsMuchAsPossibleLocked()\n\n    return qs.isIdleLocked()\n}\n```\n\n**Post-execution dispatch**: Once a request finishes, the queueset immediately attempts to dispatch waiting requests up to the concurrency limit.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Virtual Time Algorithm: Fairness in Action\n\n### 4.1 The Virtual Time Advance Rate\n\nThe **heart of fair queuing** is the virtual-time advance rate:\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) getVirtualTimeRatioLocked() float64 {\n    activeQueues := 0\n    seatsRequested := 0\n\n    for _, queue := range qs.queues {\n        seatsRequested += queue.seatsInUse + queue.requestsWaiting.QueueSum().MaxSeatsSum\n        if queue.requestsWaiting.Length() > 0 || queue.requestsExecuting.Len() > 0 {\n            activeQueues++\n        }\n    }\n\n    if activeQueues == 0 {\n        return 0  // no active queues, no virtual time advance\n    }\n\n    return math.Min(float64(seatsRequested), float64(qs.dCfg.ConcurrencyLimit)) / float64(activeQueues)\n}\n```\n\n**Interpretation**:\n- If a queue has no waiting or executing requests, it's **inactive** and doesn't participate in concurrency allocation.\n- Virtual time advances at a rate proportional to `min(total_seat_demand, ConcurrencyLimit) / num_active_queues`.\n- This ensures **fair allocation** when demand exceeds concurrency limit.\n\n**Example**:\n- `ConcurrencyLimit = 30`, `activeQueues = 3`, all at equal demand.\n- Virtual time advances at `30 / 3 = 10 seat-seconds per second`.\n- Each queue gets 1 seat-second of virtual progress per 0.1 real seconds.\n- If queue A's oldest request has work=20 seat-seconds, it will be ready at `arrivalR + 20 / 10 = arrivalR + 2 seconds`.\n\n### 4.2 Work Estimation\n\nRequests provide initial work and final work estimates:\n\n```go\ntype completedWorkEstimate struct {\n    WorkEstimate WorkEstimate\n    totalWork fqrequest.SeatSeconds  // initial + final\n    finalWork fqrequest.SeatSeconds\n}\n\nfunc (qs *queueSet) completeWorkEstimate(we *WorkEstimate) completedWorkEstimate {\n    finalWork := qs.computeFinalWork(we)\n    return completedWorkEstimate{\n        WorkEstimate: *we,\n        totalWork:    qs.computeInitialWork(we) + finalWork,\n        finalWork:    finalWork,\n    }\n}\n\nfunc (qs *queueSet) computeInitialWork(we *WorkEstimate) fqrequest.SeatSeconds {\n    return SeatsTimesDuration(float64(we.InitialSeats), qs.estimatedServiceDuration)\n}\n\nfunc (qs *queueSet) computeFinalWork(we *WorkEstimate) fqrequest.SeatSeconds {\n    return SeatsTimesDuration(float64(we.FinalSeats), we.AdditionalLatency)\n}\n```\n\n**Work = seats × duration**. A 5-seat request with 10ms estimated service duration = 50 seat-milliseconds of work.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Configuration and Reconfiguration\n\n### 5.1 Two-Phase Configuration\n\n```go\nfunc (cfgCtlr *configController) Handle(...) {\n    // Filter calls this to classify and queue\n    fs, pl, isExempt, req, startWaitingTime := cfgCtlr.startRequest(...)\n\n    // APF filter + controller interact here\n}\n```\n\nThe controller invokes:\n\n1. **`BeginConstruction`** (phase 1): Validates queuing config (shuffle-sharding parameters).\n2. **`Complete`** (phase 2): Applies the dispatching config (concurrency limit).\n\nThis allows config validation without committing concurrency allocation.\n\n### 5.2 Queue Reconfiguration\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) setConfiguration(ctx context.Context, qCfg QueuingConfig, dealer *Dealer, dCfg DispatchingConfig) {\n    qs.lockAndSyncTime(ctx)\n    defer qs.lock.Unlock()\n\n    if qCfg.DesiredNumQueues > len(qs.queues) {\n        // Add new queues\n        qs.queues = append(qs.queues, createQueues(delta, len(qs.queues))...)\n    }\n    // Note: reducing queues happens by attrition (when a queue empties and is undesired)\n\n    qs.qCfg = qCfg\n    qs.dCfg = dCfg\n    qs.dealer = dealer\n\n    qs.dispatchAsMuchAsPossibleLocked()\n}\n```\n\n**Non-disruptive changes**: Increasing queues immediately; decreasing by attrition avoids forcing drains.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Failure Modes, Limits, and Design Tradeoffs\n\n### 6.1 Overflow Protection\n\n**Issue**: Virtual time `currentR` is a 64-bit integer (seat-seconds). Over months, it can overflow.\n\n**Solution**: Epoch advancement (`advanceEpoch`).\n\n```go\nconst (\n    rDecrement = MaxSeatSeconds / 2\n    highR      = rDecrement + rDecrement / 2\n)\n\nfunc (qs *queueSet) advanceEpoch(ctx context.Context, now time.Time, incrR SeatSeconds) {\n    qs.currentR -= rDecrement\n    for _, queue := range qs.queues {\n        queue.nextDispatchR -= rDecrement\n        queue.requestsWaiting.Walk(func(req *request) bool {\n            req.arrivalR -= rDecrement\n            return true\n        })\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Tradeoff**: Epoch advancement is rare (~once per 10 years at realistic concurrency) but introduces latency spikes when it occurs. The log message is critical for monitoring.\n\n### 6.2 Seat Overage Handling\n\n**Issue**: A single request may request more seats than the concurrency limit.\n\n**Logic** (`canAccommodateSeatsLocked`):\n\n```go\nswitch {\ncase seats > ConcurrencyLimit:\n    if qs.totRequestsExecuting == 0 {\n        return true  // allow a single wide request when idle\n    }\n    return false  // wait for all current requests to finish\n}\n```\n\n**Tradeoff**: Wide requests can block the system, but they're allowed to execute (eventually), maintaining progress.\n\n### 6.3 Queue Length Limits\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) rejectOrEnqueueToBoundLocked(request *request) bool {\n    curQueueLength := queue.requestsWaiting.Length()\n    if qs.totSeatsInUse >= qs.dCfg.ConcurrencyLimit &&\n       curQueueLength >= qs.qCfg.QueueLengthLimit {\n        return false  // reject\n    }\n    qs.enqueueToBoundLocked(request)\n    return true\n}\n```\n\n**Tradeoff**: Queue length limits prevent unbounded memory growth. If set too low, bursty legitimate traffic is rejected; if too high, latency spikes occur.\n\n### 6.4 Hand Size and Collision Probability\n\nShuffle-sharding reduces collision probability. With `DesiredNumQueues = 64` and `HandSize = 8`:\n- Any two flows have a `(7/63) × (6/62) × ... ≈ 0.0005` collision probability.\n- Collisions are rare but possible; colliding flows share a queue and must wait for each other.\n\n**Tradeoff**: Larger hand size reduces collisions but increases shuffle-sharding overhead; smaller hand size is cheaper but less fair.\n\n### 6.5 Concurrency Limit Enforcement\n\nThe queueset enforces `totSeatsInUse < ConcurrencyLimit` but does **not** enforce `InitialSeats + FinalSeats = TotalSeats`. This is delegated to the work estimator.\n\n**Tradeoff**: Allows flexibility for complex work patterns (e.g., request that uses 5 seats initially, then 10 seats for finalization). Puts burden on estimator to be accurate.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Extension Points and Customization Hooks\n\n### 7.1 Promise Factory\n\n```go\ntype promiseFactory func(initial interface{}, doneCtx context.Context, doneVal interface{}) promise.WriteOnce\ntype promiseFactoryFactory func(*queueSet) promiseFactory\n```\n\nThe decision promise is customizable. This allows test injection of alternative concurrency primitives.\n\n**Use case**: Testing timeout behavior without real timeouts.\n\n### 7.2 Clock Abstraction\n\n```go\ntype eventclock.Interface interface {\n    Now() time.Time\n}\n```\n\nReal implementation: `Real{}`. Test implementation: `Fake{}`.\n\n**Use case**: Deterministic testing of epoch advancement, virtual time behavior.\n\n### 7.3 Metrics Integration\n\nEach queueset can report:\n- `reqsGaugePair.RequestsWaiting` and `RequestsExecuting`\n- `execSeatsGauge` (total seats in use)\n- `seatDemandIntegrator` (cumulative seat-demand for borrowing calculation)\n\n**Hook point**: Custom metrics observers can be injected via `TestableConfig`.\n\n### 7.4 QueueNoteFn Callback\n\n```go\ntype QueueNoteFn func(inQueue bool)\n```\n\nCalled when a request enters (`true`) and leaves (`false`) a queue. Allows filters to track queuing state.\n\n**Use case**: Observability, latency profiling.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Operational Implications\n\n### 8.1 Performance Characteristics\n\n**Lock Contention**:\n- Every `StartRequest` and `Finish` acquires the queueset lock.\n- On high-concurrency systems (1000+ RPS), lock contention can become significant.\n- The lock is held briefly (O(1) for dispatch, O(num_queues) for virtual time sync).\n\n**mitigation**: Shuffle-sharding reduces collisions; larger hand sizes help distribute load.\n\n**Memory Footprint**:\n- Each request in a queue consumes ~200-300 bytes (struct + fields).\n- Queue length limit caps memory per queueset.\n- Default limits: 64 queues × 50 queue length = up to 3200 queued requests per priority level.\n\n### 8.2 Reliability and Deadlock Avoidance\n\n**Lock Ordering**:\n- queueset lock is always acquired **before** any external calls (e.g., metrics, controller callbacks).\n- This prevents controller reentrance during queueset operations.\n\n**Context Cancellation**:\n- `StartRequest` captures the context for timeout handling.\n- If context is cancelled while waiting, the request is gracefully removed from the queue.\n- No guarantees: request might start executing just before cancellation is processed.\n\n### 8.3 Fairness Under Adversarial Load\n\n**Scenario 1: Bursty Client A, Steady Client B**\n- Client A enqueues 100 requests in 1ms.\n- Shuffle-sharding distributes A's requests across ~8 queues (hand size).\n- Each of A's queues collides with B's queue with ~0.0005 probability.\n- B's requests still get dispatched; not starved (FIFO within each queue).\n\n**Scenario 2: Wide Request (20 seats) + Many Narrow (1 seat)**\n- Wide request blocks all execution if concurrency limit is 30.\n- Narrow requests queue up; once wide request finishes, narrow requests execute in virtual-time order.\n- **Fairness property**: Wide request's virtual finish time is so high that other queues will be preferred next.\n\n### 8.4 Virtual Time Sanity\n\nThe virtual-time algorithm guarantees:\n1. **Starvation-free**: Every request eventually dispatches (no queue-switching, no priority inversion).\n2. **Proportional fairness**: If all requests have equal work, each queue gets equal virtual-time progress.\n3. **Work-aware dispatch**: Requests are ordered by `nextDispatchR + totalWork()`, not just arrival time.\n\n**Caveat**: Fairness is per-queue, not per-request. A queue with many short requests can starve a queue with fewer long requests (both queues equally fair by virtual-time, but the short-request queue has higher throughput).\n\n---\n\n## 9. Controller Integration and Priority-Level Coordination\n\n### 9.1 APF Controller Responsibilities\n\nThe controller (in `apf_controller.go`) manages:\n- FlowSchema and PriorityLevelConfiguration objects.\n- Concurrency allocation across priority levels (using `computeConcurrencyAllocation`).\n- Seat borrowing (inter-priority-level resource sharing) every 10 seconds.\n\n```go\nfunc (cfgCtlr *configController) updateBorrowingLocked(setCompleters bool, plStates map[string]*priorityLevelState) {\n    items := make([]allocProblemItem, 0, len(plStates))\n    for plName, plState := range plStates {\n        items = append(items, allocProblemItem{\n            target:     float64(plState.nominalCL),\n            lowerBound: float64(plState.minCL),\n            upperBound: float64(plState.maxCL),\n        })\n    }\n    allocs, fairFrac, err := computeConcurrencyAllocation(cfgCtlr.nominalCLSum, items)\n    // Apply allocs to each plState.currentCL\n    for idx, plName := range plNames {\n        plStates[plName].currentCL = int(allocs[idx])\n        // Call BeginConfigChange + Complete on the queueset\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Fairness at two levels**:\n1. **Inter-priority**: Concurrency allocation algorithm ensures high-priority levels get their nominal share + can borrow from unused low-priority allocation.\n2. **Intra-priority**: QueueSet's virtual time ensures flows don't starve each other within a priority level.\n\n### 9.2 Seat Demand Integration\n\nThe controller monitors `seatDemandIntegrator` (which tracks seat-demand over time):\n\n```go\ntype seatDemandStats struct {\n    avg           float64\n    stdDev        float64\n    highWatermark int\n    smoothed      float64  // exponential moving average\n}\n```\n\nThis feeds into borrowing decisions: if a priority level's demand is consistently low, it can lend concurrency to others.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Most Important Files and Directory Map\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/interface.go` | QueueSet, Request, and config interfaces |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/queueset/queueset.go` | Core queueset implementation: virtual time, dispatch, enqueue logic |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/queueset/types.go` | request, queue, completedWorkEstimate structures |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/queueset/fifo_list.go` | FIFO queue implementation and queue sum tracking |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/promise/` | WriteOnce primitive for decision promises |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/eventclock/` | Clock abstraction for testing |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/apf_filter.go` | Entry point: calls Handle() on APF Interface |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/apf_controller.go` | Config management, inter-priority-level allocation, borrowing |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/conc_alloc.go` | Concurrency allocation algorithm (proportional fairness) |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/metrics/` | Prometheus metrics integration |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/request/` | WorkEstimate and seat-seconds primitives |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/filters/priority-and-fairness.go` | HTTP filter that wraps APF interface |\n\n---\n\n## 11. Summary: Fairness Tradeoffs and Design Choices\n\n| Aspect | Design Choice | Rationale | Tradeoff |\n|--------|---------------|-----------|----------|\n| **Virtual Time** | Proportional to `min(demand, limit) / active_queues` | Fair across queues with variable demand | Bursty queues can starve steady queues (but both fair by virtual time) |\n| **Shuffle-Sharding** | Hand size = 8, choose min load | Reduce collision probability | Overhead of hand-dealing; not perfect fairness (min-load is not global optimal) |\n| **Seat-Based** | Track both request count and seat count | Support variable-width requests | Requires accurate work estimators; overflow protection needed |\n| **Queue Per Priority** | One queueset per priority level | Isolate priority levels | No direct cross-priority fairness; relies on controller's allocation algorithm |\n| **Epoch Advancement** | Subtract `rDecrement` when `currentR` exceeds threshold | Prevent 64-bit overflow | Rare but sudden latency spike; needs monitoring |\n| **Concurrency Limit** | Strict enforcement; wide requests wait | Predictable resource usage | Single wide request can block entire priority level; must allow them or starve them |\n| **FIFO Within Queue** | Oldest request first | Simplicity; prevents indefinite postponement | New short requests can't preempt long requests waiting in the same queue |\n| **Lock-Per-QueueSet** | Single mutex guarding all state | Simplicity; no per-queue locks | Lock contention at high RPS; shuffle-sharding helps but not a complete solution |\n\n---\n\n## Conclusion\n\nThe Kubernetes APF QueueSet is a sophisticated fair-queuing system that balances **simplicity**, **predictability**, and **fairness**. By coupling:\n- **Shuffle-sharded sub-queues** (reduce collisions),\n- **Virtual-time dispatch** (fair progress across variable work),\n- **Seat-based concurrency** (honor request complexity),\n- **Two-level fairness** (inter-priority via controller, intra-priority via queueset), and\n- **Overflow protection** (epoch advancement),\n\nit achieves reasonable fairness properties under diverse request patterns. The main limitations are lock contention at very high RPS and the assumption of accurate work estimators. Operators should monitor queue depths, virtual-time advance rates, and seat utilization to tune queue parameters for their workload.\n\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":10,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3914,"cache_read_input_tokens":86681,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":3914},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d26a97b2-857e-402d-abcc-7381ea12372f","uuid":"5f4cc1ed-9bc0-4a6d-a10b-f56bc16c561c"}

The **QueueSet** is the core component responsible for queuing and dispatching individual requests within a priority level, using virtual-time-based fair queuing to ensure that high-variance request patterns don't starve lower-priority flows.\n\nThis document provides an algorithmic analysis of QueueSet behavior, end-to-end control flow, and the fairness tradeoffs embedded in the design.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Subsystem Purpose, Boundaries, and Dependencies\n\n### Purpose\n\nThe APF QueueSet addresses a critical problem in multi-tenant or shared API servers: **head-of-line blocking** and **uncontrolled bursting** at the per-priority-level scale. Without QueueSet:\n- A single misbehaving user (via one FlowSchema) could create an unbounded queue of requests that starves all other flows at that priority level.\n- Virtual time doesn't progress fairly when requests have variable processing duration.\n- No mechanism exists to enforce **seat-based concurrency limits** (where a request can occupy multiple execution slots based on estimated work).\n\nThe QueueSet solves this by:\n1. **Shuffle-sharding** incoming requests into sub-queues to reduce collision probability.\n2. **Dispatching** using virtual time to ensure fair progress (weighted by request work estimates).\n3. **Enforcing concurrency limits** across multiple \"seats\" (execution parallelism units).\n\n### Boundaries\n\n**Upstream Dependencies:**\n- **APF Filter** (`apf_filter.go`): Entry point that calls `Handle()` on the APF Interface.\n- **APF Controller** (`apf_controller.go`): Manages PriorityLevelConfiguration and FlowSchema objects; creates and reconfigures QueueSets.\n- **Request classification**: FlowSchema and DistinguisherMethod determine which QueueSet a request enters.\n\n**Downstream Responsibilities:**\n- Each **non-exempt PriorityLevelConfiguration** gets one QueueSet instance.\n- **Exempt** requests (negative DesiredNumQueues) bypass all queuing and proceed directly.\n- QueueSet returns a `Request` handle; the filter calls `Request.Finish(execute)` to dispatch or reject.\n\n**Internal Scope:**\n- QueueSet is **not responsible for** flow-schema matching, rate-limiting above the priority level, or inter-priority-level borrowing (controller handles that).\n- QueueSet is **responsible for** all queuing, shuffle-sharding, virtual-time bookkeeping, and per-queue dispatching within a single priority level.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Key Components and Responsibility Split\n\n### 2.1 QueueSet Interface (`interface.go:59-97`)\n\nThe QueueSet contract defines:\n\n```\ntype QueueSet interface {\n    BeginConfigChange(QueuingConfig) (QueueSetCompleter, error)\n    IsIdle() bool\n    StartRequest(ctx, width, hashValue, flowDistinguisher, fsName, descr1, descr2, queueNoteFn) (Request, bool)\n    Dump(includeRequestDetails bool) debug.QueueSetDump\n}\n```\n\n- **`StartRequest`** is the hot path: called on every incoming request, returns a `Request` handle or `nil` if rejected.\n- **`Finish`** on the returned Request decides execution vs. rejection.\n- **Two-phase config** (`BeginConfigChange` + `Complete`) allows validation before applying changes.\n\n### 2.2 QueueSet Implementation (`queueset/queueset.go`)\n\n#### Struct Fields (Read-Only Before Lock)\n\n```go\ntype queueSet struct {\n    clock                    eventclock.Interface\n    estimatedServiceDuration time.Duration  // 3ms default; used to compute final work\n    reqsGaugePair            metrics.RatioedGaugePair\n    execSeatsGauge           metrics.RatioedGauge\n    seatDemandIntegrator     metrics.Gauge\n    promiseFactory           promiseFactory\n    lock                     sync.Mutex\n    // ... mutable fields follow\n}\n```\n\n#### Struct Fields (Guarded by Lock)\n\n```go\ntype queueSet struct {\n    // ... read-only fields ...\n    lock                     sync.Mutex\n\n    qCfg                     fq.QueuingConfig      // num queues, queue length limit, hand size\n    dCfg                     fq.DispatchingConfig  // concurrency limit\n    dealer                   *shufflesharding.Dealer\n    queues                   []*queue              // array of sub-queues\n\n    currentR                 fqrequest.SeatSeconds // virtual time (progress meter)\n    lastRealTime             time.Time\n    robinIndex               int                   // round-robin dispatch position\n\n    totRequestsWaiting       int\n    totRequestsExecuting     int\n    requestsExecutingSet     sets.Set[*request]    // used when queues is empty\n    totSeatsInUse            int\n    totSeatsWaiting          int\n\n    enqueues                 int  // counter for shuffle-sharding offset\n    totRequestsDispatched    int\n    totRequestsRejected      int\n    totRequestsTimedout      int\n    totRequestsCancelled     int\n}\n```\n\n**Key insight**: The queueSet tracks both **request count** (for backward compatibility metrics) and **seat count** (for work-aware concurrency control).\n\n#### Queue Sub-Structure (`types.go:93-110`)\n\nEach queue is a FIFO with virtual-time bookkeeping:\n\n```go\ntype queue struct {\n    requestsWaiting fifo                    // FIFO list of waiting requests\n    nextDispatchR   fqrequest.SeatSeconds   // virtual time for next dispatch\n    requestsExecuting sets.Set[*request]    // executing requests in this queue\n    index           int                     // position in queueSet.queues\n    seatsInUse      int                     // total seats occupied by executing requests\n}\n```\n\n### 2.3 Request Wrapper (`types.go:31-83`)\n\n```go\ntype request struct {\n    ctx context.Context\n    qs  *queueSet\n\n    flowDistinguisher string\n    fsName            string\n    queue             *queue\n\n    workEstimate      completedWorkEstimate // initial, final, total seats\n    decision          promise.WriteOnce     // executes or rejects\n    arrivalTime       time.Time\n    arrivalR          fqrequest.SeatSeconds // virtual time at arrival\n\n    startTime         time.Time\n    waitStarted       bool\n    removeFromQueueLocked removeFromFIFOFunc\n\n    descr1, descr2 interface{}\n    queueNoteFn    fq.QueueNoteFn\n}\n```\n\nThe `decision` field is a **WriteOnce promise**: set exactly once (by dispatcher), read by the request handler.\n\n### 2.4 Shuffle-Sharding Dealer (`queueset.go:583-612`)\n\nShuffle-sharding picks the **least-loaded queue** among a random hand:\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) shuffleShardLocked(hashValue uint64, descr1, descr2 interface{}) int {\n    hand := qs.dealer.DealIntoHand(hashValue, backHand[:])\n    handSize := len(hand)\n    offset := qs.enqueues % handSize  // offset for additional diversity\n\n    minQueueSeatSeconds := MaxSeatSeconds\n    for i := 0; i < handSize; i++ {\n        queueIdx := hand[(offset+i)%handSize]\n        queue := qs.queues[queueIdx]\n        queueSum := queue.requestsWaiting.QueueSum()\n\n        if queueSum.TotalWorkSum < minQueueSeatSeconds {\n            minQueueSeatSeconds = queueSum.TotalWorkSum\n            bestQueueIdx = queueIdx\n        }\n    }\n    return bestQueueIdx\n}\n```\n\n**Insight**: Choosing the **least-loaded queue** (by total work, not request count) ensures balanced work distribution. The hash-based hand-dealing reduces collisions for flows with similar hash values.\n\n### 2.5 Virtual Time Mechanism (`queueset.go:461-479, 489-522`)\n\nVirtual time is the **cornerstone of fair queuing**.\n\n#### Time Sync (called at lock acquisition)\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) syncTimeLocked(ctx context.Context) {\n    realNow := qs.clock.Now()\n    timeSinceLast := realNow.Sub(qs.lastRealTime)\n    qs.lastRealTime = realNow\n\n    prevR := qs.currentR\n    incrR := SeatsTimesDuration(qs.getVirtualTimeRatioLocked(), timeSinceLast)\n    qs.currentR = prevR + incrR\n\n    if qs.currentR >= highR {  // overflow prevention\n        qs.advanceEpoch(ctx, realNow, incrR)\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Virtual time increments at a rate proportional to:**\n- **Number of active queues** (those with waiting or executing requests)\n- **Concurrency limit** vs. **seat demand**\n\nThe ratio: `min(totalSeatsRequested, ConcurrencyLimit) / activeQueues`\n\nThis ensures that if all queues demand work equally, virtual time advances at the same rate for each.\n\n#### Epoch Management (Overflow Handling)\n\nWhen `currentR` exceeds `highR = rDecrement + rDecrement/2`:\n- Subtract `rDecrement` from `currentR` and all queue/request arrival times.\n- This prevents 64-bit overflow while maintaining relative ordering.\n\n---\n\n## 3. End-to-End Control and Request Flow\n\n### 3.1 Request Arrival: `StartRequest` (3 Steps)\n\n#### Step 0: Zero Queues (Direct Dispatch)\n\nIf `DesiredNumQueues < 1` (exempt or zero queues):\n- Check if request seats can fit: `totSeatsInUse + width > ConcurrencyLimit` → **reject**.\n- Otherwise, call `dispatchSansQueueLocked()` and return the request immediately.\n- No queuing overhead.\n\n#### Step 1: Shuffle-Shard and Enqueue\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) StartRequest(ctx context.Context, workEstimate *WorkEstimate, hashValue uint64, ...) (Request, bool) {\n    qs.lockAndSyncTime(ctx)  // update virtual time\n    defer qs.lock.Unlock()\n\n    queueIdx := qs.shuffleShardLocked(hashValue, descr1, descr2)\n    req := qs.shuffleShardAndRejectOrEnqueueLocked(...)\n    if req == nil {\n        return nil, qs.isIdleLocked()  // rejected: queue full AND at concurrency limit\n    }\n\n    // Step 2: Dispatch as much as possible\n    qs.dispatchAsMuchAsPossibleLocked()\n    return req, false\n}\n```\n\n**Enqueue Logic** (`rejectOrEnqueueToBoundLocked`):\n- Reject if **both**: `totSeatsInUse >= ConcurrencyLimit` **AND** `queueLength >= QueueLengthLimit`.\n- Otherwise, enqueue and initialize `queue.nextDispatchR = currentR` if queue was empty.\n\n**Metrics Updated:**\n- `totRequestsWaiting++`\n- `totSeatsWaiting += req.MaxSeats()`\n- `reqsGaugePair.RequestsWaiting.Add(1)`\n- `seatDemandIntegrator.Set(totSeatsInUse + totSeatsWaiting)`\n\n#### Step 2: Dispatch Loop\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) dispatchAsMuchAsPossibleLocked() {\n    for qs.totRequestsWaiting != 0 &&\n        qs.totSeatsInUse < qs.dCfg.ConcurrencyLimit &&\n        qs.dispatchLocked() {\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Loop condition**: While there are waiting requests, concurrency available, and we can find a dispatchable request.\n\n### 3.2 Dispatch: Fair Queuing Selection\n\n#### Queue Selection (`findDispatchQueueToBoundLocked`)\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) findDispatchQueueToBoundLocked() (*queue, *request) {\n    minVirtualFinish := MaxSeatSeconds\n\n    for range qs.queues {\n        qs.robinIndex = (qs.robinIndex + 1) % len(qs.queues)\n        queue := qs.queues[qs.robinIndex]\n\n        oldestWaiting, _ := queue.requestsWaiting.Peek()\n        if oldestWaiting != nil {\n            // virtual finish time = queue's start point + request's work\n            currentVirtualFinish := queue.nextDispatchR + oldestWaiting.totalWork()\n\n            if currentVirtualFinish < minVirtualFinish {\n                minVirtualFinish = currentVirtualFinish\n                minQueue = queue\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    return minQueue, minQueue.requestsWaiting.Peek()[0]\n}\n```\n\n**Key property**: Pick the queue whose **oldest request** has the **minimum virtual finish time**.\n\nThis ensures:\n- Requests finish in order of virtual time (FIFO within each queue).\n- Queues with less cumulative work get to execute sooner.\n- High-variance requests don't starve others.\n\n#### Request Dispatch (`dispatchLocked`)\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) dispatchLocked() bool {\n    queue, request := qs.findDispatchQueueToBoundLocked()\n    if queue == nil {\n        return false\n    }\n\n    qs.totRequestsWaiting--\n    qs.totSeatsWaiting -= request.MaxSeats()\n    request.NoteQueued(false)  // notify filter\n\n    // Set the decision to EXECUTE\n    if !request.decision.Set(decisionExecute) {\n        return true  // context was cancelled; request already abandoned\n    }\n\n    request.startTime = qs.clock.Now()\n\n    // Move from waiting to executing\n    qs.totRequestsExecuting++\n    qs.totSeatsInUse += request.MaxSeats()\n    queue.requestsExecuting = queue.requestsExecuting.Insert(request)\n    queue.seatsInUse += request.MaxSeats()\n\n    // Advance virtual finish time\n    queue.nextDispatchR += request.totalWork()\n\n    return true\n}\n```\n\n**Virtual time advance**: `queue.nextDispatchR += request.totalWork()`\n\nThis is critical: the next request in this queue won't dispatch until its virtual finish time equals or exceeds the current queue's `nextDispatchR`.\n\n### 3.3 Request Execution and Completion: `Request.Finish`\n\n#### Waiting Phase\n\n```go\nfunc (req *request) wait() (bool, bool) {\n    decisionAny := req.decision.Get()  // blocks until dispatcher sets it\n    qs := req.qs\n    qs.lockAndSyncTime(req.ctx)\n    defer qs.lock.Unlock()\n\n    switch decisionAny {\n    case decisionExecute:\n        return true, false\n    case decisionCancel:\n        // request context timed out or was cancelled while waiting\n        if req.removeFromQueueLocked() != nil {\n            qs.totRequestsWaiting--\n            qs.totSeatsWaiting -= req.MaxSeats()\n            qs.totRequestsCancelled++\n        }\n        return false, qs.isIdleLocked()\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Context Cancellation Handling**: If the client cancels while waiting, the request is removed from the queue before it starts executing. The `decision.Set()` call in `dispatchLocked` may fail if the context is already done.\n\n#### Finish and Re-Dispatch\n\n```go\nfunc (req *request) Finish(execFn func()) bool {\n    exec, idle := req.wait()\n    if !exec {\n        return idle  // was rejected or cancelled\n    }\n\n    func() {\n        defer func() {\n            idle = req.qs.finishRequestAndDispatchAsMuchAsPossible(req)\n        }()\n        execFn()  // execute the user's request\n    }()\n\n    return idle\n}\n\nfunc (qs *queueSet) finishRequestAndDispatchAsMuchAsPossible(req *request) bool {\n    qs.lockAndSyncTime(req.ctx)\n    defer qs.lock.Unlock()\n\n    // Remove from executing\n    queue := req.queue\n    if queue != nil {\n        queue.requestsExecuting = queue.requestsExecuting.Delete(req)\n        queue.seatsInUse -= req.MaxSeats()\n    } else {\n        qs.requestsExecutingSet = qs.requestsExecutingSet.Delete(req)\n    }\n    qs.totRequestsExecuting--\n    qs.totSeatsInUse -= req.MaxSeats()\n\n    // Update metrics\n    metrics.AddSeatConcurrencyInUse(qs.qCfg.Name, req.fsName, -req.MaxSeats())\n\n    // Try to dispatch more\n    qs.dispatchAsMuchAsPossibleLocked()\n\n    return qs.isIdleLocked()\n}\n```\n\n**Post-execution dispatch**: Once a request finishes, the queueset immediately attempts to dispatch waiting requests up to the concurrency limit.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Virtual Time Algorithm: Fairness in Action\n\n### 4.1 The Virtual Time Advance Rate\n\nThe **heart of fair queuing** is the virtual-time advance rate:\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) getVirtualTimeRatioLocked() float64 {\n    activeQueues := 0\n    seatsRequested := 0\n\n    for _, queue := range qs.queues {\n        seatsRequested += queue.seatsInUse + queue.requestsWaiting.QueueSum().MaxSeatsSum\n        if queue.requestsWaiting.Length() > 0 || queue.requestsExecuting.Len() > 0 {\n            activeQueues++\n        }\n    }\n\n    if activeQueues == 0 {\n        return 0  // no active queues, no virtual time advance\n    }\n\n    return math.Min(float64(seatsRequested), float64(qs.dCfg.ConcurrencyLimit)) / float64(activeQueues)\n}\n```\n\n**Interpretation**:\n- If a queue has no waiting or executing requests, it's **inactive** and doesn't participate in concurrency allocation.\n- Virtual time advances at a rate proportional to `min(total_seat_demand, ConcurrencyLimit) / num_active_queues`.\n- This ensures **fair allocation** when demand exceeds concurrency limit.\n\n**Example**:\n- `ConcurrencyLimit = 30`, `activeQueues = 3`, all at equal demand.\n- Virtual time advances at `30 / 3 = 10 seat-seconds per second`.\n- Each queue gets 1 seat-second of virtual progress per 0.1 real seconds.\n- If queue A's oldest request has work=20 seat-seconds, it will be ready at `arrivalR + 20 / 10 = arrivalR + 2 seconds`.\n\n### 4.2 Work Estimation\n\nRequests provide initial work and final work estimates:\n\n```go\ntype completedWorkEstimate struct {\n    WorkEstimate WorkEstimate\n    totalWork fqrequest.SeatSeconds  // initial + final\n    finalWork fqrequest.SeatSeconds\n}\n\nfunc (qs *queueSet) completeWorkEstimate(we *WorkEstimate) completedWorkEstimate {\n    finalWork := qs.computeFinalWork(we)\n    return completedWorkEstimate{\n        WorkEstimate: *we,\n        totalWork:    qs.computeInitialWork(we) + finalWork,\n        finalWork:    finalWork,\n    }\n}\n\nfunc (qs *queueSet) computeInitialWork(we *WorkEstimate) fqrequest.SeatSeconds {\n    return SeatsTimesDuration(float64(we.InitialSeats), qs.estimatedServiceDuration)\n}\n\nfunc (qs *queueSet) computeFinalWork(we *WorkEstimate) fqrequest.SeatSeconds {\n    return SeatsTimesDuration(float64(we.FinalSeats), we.AdditionalLatency)\n}\n```\n\n**Work = seats × duration**. A 5-seat request with 10ms estimated service duration = 50 seat-milliseconds of work.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Configuration and Reconfiguration\n\n### 5.1 Two-Phase Configuration\n\n```go\nfunc (cfgCtlr *configController) Handle(...) {\n    // Filter calls this to classify and queue\n    fs, pl, isExempt, req, startWaitingTime := cfgCtlr.startRequest(...)\n\n    // APF filter + controller interact here\n}\n```\n\nThe controller invokes:\n\n1. **`BeginConstruction`** (phase 1): Validates queuing config (shuffle-sharding parameters).\n2. **`Complete`** (phase 2): Applies the dispatching config (concurrency limit).\n\nThis allows config validation without committing concurrency allocation.\n\n### 5.2 Queue Reconfiguration\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) setConfiguration(ctx context.Context, qCfg QueuingConfig, dealer *Dealer, dCfg DispatchingConfig) {\n    qs.lockAndSyncTime(ctx)\n    defer qs.lock.Unlock()\n\n    if qCfg.DesiredNumQueues > len(qs.queues) {\n        // Add new queues\n        qs.queues = append(qs.queues, createQueues(delta, len(qs.queues))...)\n    }\n    // Note: reducing queues happens by attrition (when a queue empties and is undesired)\n\n    qs.qCfg = qCfg\n    qs.dCfg = dCfg\n    qs.dealer = dealer\n\n    qs.dispatchAsMuchAsPossibleLocked()\n}\n```\n\n**Non-disruptive changes**: Increasing queues immediately; decreasing by attrition avoids forcing drains.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Failure Modes, Limits, and Design Tradeoffs\n\n### 6.1 Overflow Protection\n\n**Issue**: Virtual time `currentR` is a 64-bit integer (seat-seconds). Over months, it can overflow.\n\n**Solution**: Epoch advancement (`advanceEpoch`).\n\n```go\nconst (\n    rDecrement = MaxSeatSeconds / 2\n    highR      = rDecrement + rDecrement / 2\n)\n\nfunc (qs *queueSet) advanceEpoch(ctx context.Context, now time.Time, incrR SeatSeconds) {\n    qs.currentR -= rDecrement\n    for _, queue := range qs.queues {\n        queue.nextDispatchR -= rDecrement\n        queue.requestsWaiting.Walk(func(req *request) bool {\n            req.arrivalR -= rDecrement\n            return true\n        })\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Tradeoff**: Epoch advancement is rare (~once per 10 years at realistic concurrency) but introduces latency spikes when it occurs. The log message is critical for monitoring.\n\n### 6.2 Seat Overage Handling\n\n**Issue**: A single request may request more seats than the concurrency limit.\n\n**Logic** (`canAccommodateSeatsLocked`):\n\n```go\nswitch {\ncase seats > ConcurrencyLimit:\n    if qs.totRequestsExecuting == 0 {\n        return true  // allow a single wide request when idle\n    }\n    return false  // wait for all current requests to finish\n}\n```\n\n**Tradeoff**: Wide requests can block the system, but they're allowed to execute (eventually), maintaining progress.\n\n### 6.3 Queue Length Limits\n\n```go\nfunc (qs *queueSet) rejectOrEnqueueToBoundLocked(request *request) bool {\n    curQueueLength := queue.requestsWaiting.Length()\n    if qs.totSeatsInUse >= qs.dCfg.ConcurrencyLimit &&\n       curQueueLength >= qs.qCfg.QueueLengthLimit {\n        return false  // reject\n    }\n    qs.enqueueToBoundLocked(request)\n    return true\n}\n```\n\n**Tradeoff**: Queue length limits prevent unbounded memory growth. If set too low, bursty legitimate traffic is rejected; if too high, latency spikes occur.\n\n### 6.4 Hand Size and Collision Probability\n\nShuffle-sharding reduces collision probability. With `DesiredNumQueues = 64` and `HandSize = 8`:\n- Any two flows have a `(7/63) × (6/62) × ... ≈ 0.0005` collision probability.\n- Collisions are rare but possible; colliding flows share a queue and must wait for each other.\n\n**Tradeoff**: Larger hand size reduces collisions but increases shuffle-sharding overhead; smaller hand size is cheaper but less fair.\n\n### 6.5 Concurrency Limit Enforcement\n\nThe queueset enforces `totSeatsInUse < ConcurrencyLimit` but does **not** enforce `InitialSeats + FinalSeats = TotalSeats`. This is delegated to the work estimator.\n\n**Tradeoff**: Allows flexibility for complex work patterns (e.g., request that uses 5 seats initially, then 10 seats for finalization). Puts burden on estimator to be accurate.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Extension Points and Customization Hooks\n\n### 7.1 Promise Factory\n\n```go\ntype promiseFactory func(initial interface{}, doneCtx context.Context, doneVal interface{}) promise.WriteOnce\ntype promiseFactoryFactory func(*queueSet) promiseFactory\n```\n\nThe decision promise is customizable. This allows test injection of alternative concurrency primitives.\n\n**Use case**: Testing timeout behavior without real timeouts.\n\n### 7.2 Clock Abstraction\n\n```go\ntype eventclock.Interface interface {\n    Now() time.Time\n}\n```\n\nReal implementation: `Real{}`. Test implementation: `Fake{}`.\n\n**Use case**: Deterministic testing of epoch advancement, virtual time behavior.\n\n### 7.3 Metrics Integration\n\nEach queueset can report:\n- `reqsGaugePair.RequestsWaiting` and `RequestsExecuting`\n- `execSeatsGauge` (total seats in use)\n- `seatDemandIntegrator` (cumulative seat-demand for borrowing calculation)\n\n**Hook point**: Custom metrics observers can be injected via `TestableConfig`.\n\n### 7.4 QueueNoteFn Callback\n\n```go\ntype QueueNoteFn func(inQueue bool)\n```\n\nCalled when a request enters (`true`) and leaves (`false`) a queue. Allows filters to track queuing state.\n\n**Use case**: Observability, latency profiling.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Operational Implications\n\n### 8.1 Performance Characteristics\n\n**Lock Contention**:\n- Every `StartRequest` and `Finish` acquires the queueset lock.\n- On high-concurrency systems (1000+ RPS), lock contention can become significant.\n- The lock is held briefly (O(1) for dispatch, O(num_queues) for virtual time sync).\n\n**mitigation**: Shuffle-sharding reduces collisions; larger hand sizes help distribute load.\n\n**Memory Footprint**:\n- Each request in a queue consumes ~200-300 bytes (struct + fields).\n- Queue length limit caps memory per queueset.\n- Default limits: 64 queues × 50 queue length = up to 3200 queued requests per priority level.\n\n### 8.2 Reliability and Deadlock Avoidance\n\n**Lock Ordering**:\n- queueset lock is always acquired **before** any external calls (e.g., metrics, controller callbacks).\n- This prevents controller reentrance during queueset operations.\n\n**Context Cancellation**:\n- `StartRequest` captures the context for timeout handling.\n- If context is cancelled while waiting, the request is gracefully removed from the queue.\n- No guarantees: request might start executing just before cancellation is processed.\n\n### 8.3 Fairness Under Adversarial Load\n\n**Scenario 1: Bursty Client A, Steady Client B**\n- Client A enqueues 100 requests in 1ms.\n- Shuffle-sharding distributes A's requests across ~8 queues (hand size).\n- Each of A's queues collides with B's queue with ~0.0005 probability.\n- B's requests still get dispatched; not starved (FIFO within each queue).\n\n**Scenario 2: Wide Request (20 seats) + Many Narrow (1 seat)**\n- Wide request blocks all execution if concurrency limit is 30.\n- Narrow requests queue up; once wide request finishes, narrow requests execute in virtual-time order.\n- **Fairness property**: Wide request's virtual finish time is so high that other queues will be preferred next.\n\n### 8.4 Virtual Time Sanity\n\nThe virtual-time algorithm guarantees:\n1. **Starvation-free**: Every request eventually dispatches (no queue-switching, no priority inversion).\n2. **Proportional fairness**: If all requests have equal work, each queue gets equal virtual-time progress.\n3. **Work-aware dispatch**: Requests are ordered by `nextDispatchR + totalWork()`, not just arrival time.\n\n**Caveat**: Fairness is per-queue, not per-request. A queue with many short requests can starve a queue with fewer long requests (both queues equally fair by virtual-time, but the short-request queue has higher throughput).\n\n---\n\n## 9. Controller Integration and Priority-Level Coordination\n\n### 9.1 APF Controller Responsibilities\n\nThe controller (in `apf_controller.go`) manages:\n- FlowSchema and PriorityLevelConfiguration objects.\n- Concurrency allocation across priority levels (using `computeConcurrencyAllocation`).\n- Seat borrowing (inter-priority-level resource sharing) every 10 seconds.\n\n```go\nfunc (cfgCtlr *configController) updateBorrowingLocked(setCompleters bool, plStates map[string]*priorityLevelState) {\n    items := make([]allocProblemItem, 0, len(plStates))\n    for plName, plState := range plStates {\n        items = append(items, allocProblemItem{\n            target:     float64(plState.nominalCL),\n            lowerBound: float64(plState.minCL),\n            upperBound: float64(plState.maxCL),\n        })\n    }\n    allocs, fairFrac, err := computeConcurrencyAllocation(cfgCtlr.nominalCLSum, items)\n    // Apply allocs to each plState.currentCL\n    for idx, plName := range plNames {\n        plStates[plName].currentCL = int(allocs[idx])\n        // Call BeginConfigChange + Complete on the queueset\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Fairness at two levels**:\n1. **Inter-priority**: Concurrency allocation algorithm ensures high-priority levels get their nominal share + can borrow from unused low-priority allocation.\n2. **Intra-priority**: QueueSet's virtual time ensures flows don't starve each other within a priority level.\n\n### 9.2 Seat Demand Integration\n\nThe controller monitors `seatDemandIntegrator` (which tracks seat-demand over time):\n\n```go\ntype seatDemandStats struct {\n    avg           float64\n    stdDev        float64\n    highWatermark int\n    smoothed      float64  // exponential moving average\n}\n```\n\nThis feeds into borrowing decisions: if a priority level's demand is consistently low, it can lend concurrency to others.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Most Important Files and Directory Map\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/interface.go` | QueueSet, Request, and config interfaces |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/queueset/queueset.go` | Core queueset implementation: virtual time, dispatch, enqueue logic |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/queueset/types.go` | request, queue, completedWorkEstimate structures |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/queueset/fifo_list.go` | FIFO queue implementation and queue sum tracking |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/promise/` | WriteOnce primitive for decision promises |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/eventclock/` | Clock abstraction for testing |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/apf_filter.go` | Entry point: calls Handle() on APF Interface |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/apf_controller.go` | Config management, inter-priority-level allocation, borrowing |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/conc_alloc.go` | Concurrency allocation algorithm (proportional fairness) |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/metrics/` | Prometheus metrics integration |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/request/` | WorkEstimate and seat-seconds primitives |\n| `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/filters/priority-and-fairness.go` | HTTP filter that wraps APF interface |\n\n---\n\n## 11. Summary: Fairness Tradeoffs and Design Choices\n\n| Aspect | Design Choice | Rationale | Tradeoff |\n|--------|---------------|-----------|----------|\n| **Virtual Time** | Proportional to `min(demand, limit) / active_queues` | Fair across queues with variable demand | Bursty queues can starve steady queues (but both fair by virtual time) |\n| **Shuffle-Sharding** | Hand size = 8, choose min load | Reduce collision probability | Overhead of hand-dealing; not perfect fairness (min-load is not global optimal) |\n| **Seat-Based** | Track both request count and seat count | Support variable-width requests | Requires accurate work estimators; overflow protection needed |\n| **Queue Per Priority** | One queueset per priority level | Isolate priority levels | No direct cross-priority fairness; relies on controller's allocation algorithm |\n| **Epoch Advancement** | Subtract `rDecrement` when `currentR` exceeds threshold | Prevent 64-bit overflow | Rare but sudden latency spike; needs monitoring |\n| **Concurrency Limit** | Strict enforcement; wide requests wait | Predictable resource usage | Single wide request can block entire priority level; must allow them or starve them |\n| **FIFO Within Queue** | Oldest request first | Simplicity; prevents indefinite postponement | New short requests can't preempt long requests waiting in the same queue |\n| **Lock-Per-QueueSet** | Single mutex guarding all state | Simplicity; no per-queue locks | Lock contention at high RPS; shuffle-sharding helps but not a complete solution |\n\n---\n\n## Conclusion\n\nThe Kubernetes APF QueueSet is a sophisticated fair-queuing system that balances **simplicity**, **predictability**, and **fairness**. By coupling:\n- **Shuffle-sharded sub-queues** (reduce collisions),\n- **Virtual-time dispatch** (fair progress across variable work),\n- **Seat-based concurrency** (honor request complexity),\n- **Two-level fairness** (inter-priority via controller, intra-priority via queueset), and\n- **Overflow protection** (epoch advancement),\n\nit achieves reasonable fairness properties under diverse request patterns. The main limitations are lock contention at very high RPS and the assumption of accurate work estimators. Operators should monitor queue depths, virtual-time advance rates, and seat utilization to tune queue parameters for their workload.\n\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
